Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.

It feels embarrassing to say out loud, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of goes offline. I’ll be reaching for more food while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And then I still keep going. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I wish I could say I was. Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think that is what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating this way as a way to cope with stress or emotional upset, especially after a tense night with your husband?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I mean… I know the tense nights don’t help, and that does seem to be when it happens more. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m consciously using food to “cope,” if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more like I’m upset or wound up, and then eating just kind of happens before I’ve really thought it through. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I keep telling myself it’s just leftovers or a snack after everyone’s gone to bed, but then sometimes I’ll realize I’ve eaten, like… half a tray of catered pasta, dessert from a client dinner, and whatever snacks are in the pantry, all in one sitting. It feels embarrassing to say out loud, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of goes offline. I’ll be reaching for more food while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And then I still keep going. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I wish I could say I was. Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think that is what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating this way as a way to cope with stress or emotional upset, especially after a tense night with your husband?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I mean… I know the tense nights don’t help, and that does seem to be when it happens more. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m consciously using food to “cope,” if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more like I’m upset or wound up, and then eating just kind of happens before I’ve really thought it through. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think that is what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating this way as a way to cope with stress or emotional upset, especially after a tense night with your husband?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I mean… I know the tense nights don’t help, and that does seem to be when it happens more. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m consciously using food to “cope,” if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more like I’m upset or wound up, and then eating just kind of happens before I’ve really thought it through. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. But not only that, if I’m being honest.\n\nSometimes it’s after a late work event too, when I get home keyed up and tired. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I wish I could say I was. Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. And I’ll sit there thinking, “Why didn’t you just go for a run or do yoga or something normal instead?” It’s… not a great feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating really quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll be standing at the kitchen island, or half-scrolling through work emails, and I’m eating so quickly I’m barely even tasting it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food alone, without others around?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think that is what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating this way as a way to cope with stress or emotional upset, especially after a tense night with your husband?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I mean… I know the tense nights don’t help, and that does seem to be when it happens more. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m consciously using food to “cope,” if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more like I’m upset or wound up, and then eating just kind of happens before I’ve really thought it through. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. But not only that, if I’m being honest.\n\nSometimes it’s after a late work event too, when I get home keyed up and tired. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're criticizing yourself or thinking about the consequences of eating while you're in the middle of one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, not in a very clear, organized way, but there’s definitely a running thought of, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.”\n\nAnd I am aware, even while I’m doing it, that I’m going to feel awful about it afterward. It’s just… once I’ve started, especially after things have been tense with my husband, it feels like that sensible part of my brain kind of goes offline. I can be criticizing myself and still reaching for more, which is embarrassing to admit. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think that is what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating this way as a way to cope with stress or emotional upset, especially after a tense night with your husband?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I mean… I know the tense nights don’t help, and that does seem to be when it happens more. But I don’t know if I’d say I’m consciously using food to “cope,” if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more like I’m upset or wound up, and then eating just kind of happens before I’ve really thought it through. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. But not only that, if I’m being honest.\n\nSometimes it’s after a late work event too, when I get home keyed up and tired. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're criticizing yourself or thinking about the consequences of eating while you're in the middle of one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, not in a very clear, organized way, but there’s definitely a running thought of, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.”\n\nAnd I am aware, even while I’m doing it, that I’m going to feel awful about it afterward. It’s just… once I’ve started, especially after things have been tense with my husband, it feels like that sensible part of my brain kind of goes offline. I can be criticizing myself and still reaching for more, which is embarrassing to admit. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your eating during these episodes because you're trying to calm down or distract yourself from your emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair. I don’t want to make it sound like I have no responsibility for it, but in the moment it does feel like I’m trying to quiet everything down.\n\nEspecially after a tense night with my husband, once I start eating, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll even be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think it is connected to trying to calm myself or not feel what I’m feeling right then. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating at night happened frequently enough that you feel like they're a regular part of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I’d have to say it has become pretty regular, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nFor the past three months or so, there’s been at least one night almost every week where I end up eating like that—usually after an argument or a late work event. And some weeks it’s happened twice. I don’t know if that counts as “regular” in the way you mean, but it does feel like it’s become part of the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that worries me most, honestly.\n\nI don’t want to say I have absolutely no control, because that sounds so dramatic, but once I start—especially after a tense night with my husband—it really does feel like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I still keep reaching for more.\n\nSo… yes, in those moments it doesn’t feel like I’m fully in control. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think it is directly tied to that, at least most of the time.

I don’t want to blame the arguments completely, because I know I’m still the one doing it, but after a tense night with my husband, once I start eating, it’s like my decision-making just sort of goes offline. I can literally be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more.

So yes, the emotional upset does seem to trigger that loss of control. Does that make sense?

It feels embarrassing to say out loud, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, that’s enough,” and somehow I’m still reaching for more. It sounds awful when I say it, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I try not to make a huge thing of it, because I know people overeat sometimes, but afterward I feel really embarrassed. Sometimes disgusted with myself, honestly. Like I’ve ruined this disciplined person I’m supposed to be, or that I try so hard to be. I mean, not every single time, but often enough that I’ve noticed it. Then I look down and the containers are empty, and it’s like I didn’t fully register what I was doing until afterward. I mean, a lot of the time it’s late, so people just aren’t really around, you know? I wouldn’t say I’m making some big plan to hide it or anything… at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more that if my husband is asleep or occupied, that’s when it happens. I guess I’d rather not have anyone watching me, but… I don’t know if that counts as “alone” in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more rapidly than usual during these episodes because you feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, there may be some connection there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, thankfully. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But… over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night most weeks where it happens, usually after an argument or a late work event.\n\nSome weeks it’s happened twice. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're criticizing yourself or thinking about the consequences of eating while you're in the middle of one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It feels embarrassing to say out loud, but… that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’ll be reaching for more food while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And then I still keep going. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I wish I could say I was. Once I start, especially if things have been tense with my husband, it’s like the sensible part of me just shuts off. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just kind of… goes offline.\n\nI can be reaching for more while I’m literally thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.” And I still keep going. So yes, I guess the rapid eating is connected to that feeling that I can’t quite stop myself in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes. I don’t like saying it that strongly, because it makes me sound irresponsible, but in the moment it really does feel like I’m not fully in charge.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes reasonable decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and at the same time I’m still reaching for more. Saying that out loud feels a little embarrassing, but I think that’s the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after every one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not every single time in the exact same way, I guess… but yes, most of the time afterward I feel really embarrassed. Ashamed, too.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, “Why did you do that?” and I feel sort of disgusted with myself, like I’ve ruined this disciplined version of myself I try so hard to be. And then I start thinking I should have done something healthier, like gone for a run or done yoga instead.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s usually where my mind goes afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t love admitting that, but once I start, especially after a tense night with my husband, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off.\n\nI’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It’s not that I’m enjoying it the whole time, exactly. It just feels hard to interrupt once it’s started. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. But not only that, if I’m being honest.\n\nSometimes it’s after a late work event too, when I get home keyed up and tired. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're criticizing yourself or thinking about the consequences of eating while you're in the middle of one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, not in a very clear, organized way, but there’s definitely a running thought of, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.”\n\nAnd I am aware, even while I’m doing it, that I’m going to feel awful about it afterward. It’s just… once I’ve started, especially after things have been tense with my husband, it feels like that sensible part of my brain kind of goes offline. I can be criticizing myself and still reaching for more, which is embarrassing to admit. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your eating during these episodes because you're trying to calm down or distract yourself from your emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair. I don’t want to make it sound like I have no responsibility for it, but in the moment it does feel like I’m trying to quiet everything down.\n\nEspecially after a tense night with my husband, once I start eating, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll even be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think it is connected to trying to calm myself or not feel what I’m feeling right then. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating at night happened frequently enough that you feel like they're a regular part of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I’d have to say it has become pretty regular, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nFor the past three months or so, there’s been at least one night almost every week where I end up eating like that—usually after an argument or a late work event. Is that what you mean by unable to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes, or is it more like you're along for the ride?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s more like I’m along for the ride, though I hate saying it that way because it sounds so out of control.\n\nOnce I start, especially if it’s been a tense night with my husband, it’s like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” but then I’m still reaching for more anyway. So no, I don’t think I feel fully in control in those moments.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes only happen after arguments or tense nights with your husband?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes… after arguments or tense nights with him. But not only that, if I’m being honest.\n\nSometimes it’s after a late work event too, when I get home keyed up and tired. Over the last three months, I’d say there’s been at least one night almost every week where it happens, usually after one of those situations. And… some weeks it’s happened twice.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of pattern you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're criticizing yourself or thinking about the consequences of eating while you're in the middle of one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, not in a very clear, organized way, but there’s definitely a running thought of, “Patricia, stop, this is enough.”\n\nAnd I am aware, even while I’m doing it, that I’m going to feel awful about it afterward. It’s just… once I’ve started, especially after things have been tense with my husband, it feels like that sensible part of my brain kind of goes offline. I can be criticizing myself and still reaching for more, which is embarrassing to admit. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your eating during these episodes because you're trying to calm down or distract yourself from your emotions?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair. I don’t want to make it sound like I have no responsibility for it, but in the moment it does feel like I’m trying to quiet everything down.\n\nEspecially after a tense night with my husband, once I start eating, it’s like the part of me that makes sensible decisions just sort of shuts off. I’ll even be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more. So… yes, I think it is connected to trying to calm myself or not feel what I’m feeling right then. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating at night happened frequently enough that you feel like they're a regular part of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I’d have to say it has become pretty regular, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nFor the past three months or so, there’s been at least one night almost every week where I end up eating like that—usually after an argument or a late work event. And some weeks it’s happened twice. I don’t know if that counts as “regular” in the way you mean, but it does feel like it’s become part of the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that worries me most, honestly.\n\nI don’t want to say I have absolutely no control, because that sounds so dramatic, but once I start—especially after a tense night with my husband—it really does feel like the decision-making part of me just goes offline. I’ll be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I still keep reaching for more.\n\nSo… yes, in those moments it doesn’t feel like I’m fully in control. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the loss of control over eating during these episodes is directly triggered by the emotional upset, such as the arguments with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is directly tied to that, at least most of the time.\n\nI don’t want to blame the arguments completely, because I know I’m still the one doing it, but after a tense night with my husband, once I start eating, it’s like my decision-making just sort of goes offline. I can literally be thinking, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I’m still reaching for more.\n\nSo yes, the emotional upset does seem to trigger that loss of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you lose control over your eating only during these episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, mostly only during those bigger episodes.\n\nIt’s not like I feel that way every time I eat or anything. But when it’s one of those nights and I’ve already started, especially after things have been tense with my husband, it’s like something in me switches off. I’ll be telling myself, “Patricia, stop, this is enough,” and then I still keep reaching for more.\n\nSo yes, I guess that loss of control is really part of those episodes. Does that answer what you mean?"
